## Claude _2026-08-14 16:43:35 UTC_

Kaldığımız yerden devam. Bölüm 1'i onaylanmış sayıyorum, şimdi Bölüm 2'ye geçiyorum.

### Bölüm 2 — Mekan arama ve ekleme akışı

**Arama:** Kullanıcı konum izni verir, cihaz konumu alınır. Uygulama `mekanlar` tablosuna PostGIS `ST_DWithin` sorgusuyla "bana yakın mekanlar"ı çeker (varsayılan yarıçap, örn. 2 km), isteğe bağlı isim filtresiyle daraltılır, mesafeye göre sıralanır. Bütün sorgu kendi veritabanımızda — dış servise gitmiyor.

**Aradığı mekan listede yoksa → "Mekan ekle":**
- Form: ad, tür (kafe/bar/restoran/park/…), adres (opsiyonel, GPS'ten otomatik doldurulabilir).
- **~200 m yakınlık şartı:** cihaz konumu, eklenecek mekana bu mesafeden uzaksa form gönderilemez — kullanıcı fiilen orada olmalı. (Karar #7'deki hız kesici, duvar değil.)
- **Mükerrer kayıt önleme:** kaydetmeden önce yakın çevredeki benzer isimli mekanlar kullanıcıya gösterilir ("bunlardan biri mi demek istedin?").
- **Günlük ekleme limiti:** kötüye kullanımı frenlemek için kullanıcı başına gün içinde sınırlı sayıda mekan eklenebilir.
- Eklenen mekan `ekleyen_kullanici` alanıyla işaretlenir, moderasyon kuyruğu olmadan hemen `mekanlar` tablosuna girer ama şikayet edilebilir (Faz 2b'de tam şikayet akışı gelecek, 2a'da sadece işaretleme var mı yoksa hiç yok mu — bu bir karar noktası, aşağıda soruyorum).

Bir açık nokta: 2a'da "mekan ekle" formunda bir şikayet/bildirme butonu olsun mu, yoksa bu tamamen Faz 2b'ye mi bırakılsın (spec'teki "9. 2a'ya bilerek alinmayanlar" listesine göre şikayet zaten 2b'de)? Ben **tamamen 2b'ye bırakmayı** öneriyorum — tutarlı olur, 2a'da kimse kimseyi görmediği için şikayet edilecek bir "görünürlük" henüz yok.

Bölüm 2 bu haliyle doğru mu, onaylıyor musun?

## Claude _2026-08-14 16:44:17 UTC_

## Bölüm 1 — Veri modeli ve mekan verisinin yüklenmesi (tekrar)

**Üç tablo mantığı, iki fiziksel tablo:**

- **`mekanlar`** — OpenStreetMap'ten (Geofabrik Türkiye dosyası) tek seferlik yüklenen kalıcı mekan kataloğu, ~200-400 bin kayıt. Alanlar: ad, tür (kafe/bar/restoran/park…), `konum` (PostGIS `geography(Point)`), adres, `osm_id`, `ekleyen_kullanici` (OSM'den geldiyse boş, kullanıcı eklediyse dolu). `konum` üzerinde GiST indeksi var — "yakınımdaki mekanlar" sorgusunu hızlı tutan şey bu.

- **`check_inler`** — her check-in bir satır. Alanlar: kullanıcı, mekan, not, fotoğraf, `olusturma_zamani`, `bitis_zamani` (oluşturma + 4 saat), `profilde_kalsin` (varsayılan `false`), `konum` (nullable).

- **Ani için ayrı tablo yok.** Süresi dolmuş ama `profilde_kalsin = true` olan check-in satırı zaten "ani"dır. Yani "anıya çevir" işlemi bir kopyalama değil, tek bir alan güncellemesi (`profilde_kalsin: false → true`).

**Süre dolduğunda (4 saat sonra), bir zamanlanmış iş (pg_cron) çalışır:**
- `profilde_kalsin = false` olan satırlar **tamamen silinir**.
- `profilde_kalsin = true` olanların yalnızca **`konum` alanı null'lanır** — geri kalan (not, fotoğraf, mekan, zaman) kalır.
- Sonuç: sistemde hiçbir zaman koordinat geçmişi birikmiyor. "Konum verisi tutmuyoruz" duruşunu ayakta tutan mekanizma bu.

**OSM yüklemesi:**
- Uygulamanın çalışma zamanında değil, `araclar/` altında ayrı, tek seferlik bir betik.
- Geofabrik Türkiye dosyasını indirir, ilgili etiketleri (`amenity=cafe|bar|restaurant`, `leisure=park` vb.) süzer, `mekanlar` tablosuna toplu yazar.
- Veri tazelemek istediğimizde elle tekrar çalıştırılır.
- OSM lisansı gereği atıf uygulamada görünür olacak.

Bu haliyle netleşti mi, yoksa değiştirmek/eklemek istediğin bir şey var mı? Onaylarsan Bölüm 2'ye (mekan arama/ekleme akışı) geçelim.

Bu, Bölüm 1'deki karar #2 ve #3'ü değiştiriyor — netleştireyim ve tek bir açık noktayı sorayım.

**Değişen kısım — kalıcılık artık opt-in değil, varsayılan:**
Önceki tasarımda check-in süresi dolunca (`profilde_kalsin = false` ise) satır **silinirdi**; kullanıcı isterse önceden "anıya kaydet" diyerek kurtarırdı. Senin şimdi söylediğin: check-in süresi dolduğunda (ya da "ayrıldım" dediğinde) **otomatik olarak anıya dönüşsün**, silinmesin — silme kararı tamamen kullanıcıda, istediği zaman istediği anısını silebilir.

Bu aslında modeli sadeleştiriyor: `profilde_kalsin` bayrağına gerek kalmıyor. Yeni akış:
- Check-in oluşur → 4 saat (veya "ayrıldım" ile daha erken) canlı kalır, mekanda "şu an burada" listesinde görünür.
- Süre dolunca / ayrıldığında: `konum` alanı null'lanır, satır **anı** olur — otomatik, kullanıcı bir şey yapmasa da.
- Anı, kullanıcı elle silene kadar profilde durur.
- pg_cron artık satır **silmiyor**, sadece `konum`'u null'luyor — silme işlemi sadece kullanıcı eylemiyle oluyor.

## Claude _2026-08-14 16:58:44 UTC_

Bölüm 2'yi onaylanmış sayıyorum (şikayet/bildirme tamamen Faz 2b'ye bırakıldı). Şimdi Bölüm 3'e geçiyorum.

### Bölüm 3 — Check-in oluşturma akışı

**Check-in başlatma:**
- Kullanıcı bir mekan seçer (arama sonucundan ya da yeni eklediği mekandan).
- **~500 m yakınlık kontrolü:** cihaz konumu mekana bu mesafeden uzaksa check-in butonu devre dışı/hata verir (karar #10).
- Kullanıcı isteğe bağlı bir **not** yazar ve/veya bir **fotoğraf** ekler (video yok, karar #4).
- Check-in oluşur: `olusturma_zamani = şimdi`, `bitis_zamani = şimdi + 4 saat`, `konum = cihaz konumu`, `gorunurluk = varsayılan`.

**Canlı fazda (0–4 saat, ya da "ayrıldım"a kadar):**
- Check-in, o mekanda **aynı anda check-in yapmış diğer kişilere** görünür (karar #12) — mekan ekranının "şu an burada" bölümünde.
- Kullanıcı istediği an **"ayrıldım"** butonuna basabilir → canlı faz erken sona erer.
- Aynı anda birden fazla aktif check-in olabilir mi, yoksa yeni check-in eskisini otomatik kapatır mı? *(Bunu netleştirmemiz gerekiyor — aşağıda soruyorum.)*

**Süre dolunca / "ayrıldım" ile:**
- `konum` null'lanır, satır otomatik **anı** olur (karar #11) — profilde kalıcı, sadece kullanıcı silebilir.
- Mekan ekranının "şu an burada" listesinden düşer, mekanın "anılar" bölümüne (herkese açık anılar, karar #6) girer — burada da gerçek görünürlük mantığı Faz 2b'ye kadar `gorunurluk` alanına bakmadan "herkese açık" davranır mı, yoksa 2a'da anılar da hiç görünmez mi? *(İkinci açık nokta.)*

İki açık nokta var:
1. **Kullanıcının aynı anda birden fazla aktif (canlı) check-in'i olabilir mi?** Ben "hayır, tek aktif check-in" öneririm — biri iki farklı mekanda aynı anda "şu an buradayım" diyemez, mantıksal olarak tutarsız olur. Yeni check-in yapmak, varsa eskisini otomatik "ayrıldım" gibi kapatır.
2. **Anılar (geçmiş check-in'ler) mekan ekranında 2a'da görünür mü?** Karar #6 "mekanda paylaşılan herkese açık anılar" diyordu — ama karar #8 "2a'da kimse kimseyi görmüyor" diyordu. <result>## Spec Compliance

- ✅ Migration file created at `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260814211305_mekanlar.sql`, correct location and naming convention (timestamp prefix + snake_case description), consistent with prior Faz 1 migrations (`20260813091930_profil_fotograflari_bucket.sql`, `20260813115636_profiller.sql`).
- ✅ `create extension if not exists postgis;` present (line 1 of the new file).
- ✅ `mekanlar` table schema matches the brief's interface spec exactly, column-for-column: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `ad text not null`, `tur text not null`, `konum geography(point, 4326) not null`, `adres text`, `osm_id bigint`, `ekleyen_kullanici uuid references auth.users(id) on delete set null`, `olusturuldu timestamptz not null default now()`. All 8 brief-specified columns present, none extra, none missing.
- ✅ GIST spatial index `mekanlar_konum_idx` on `konum`, matches brief.
- ✅ RLS enabled (`alter table public.mekanlar enable row level security;`).
- ✅ Read-only policy `"herkes mekanlari okuyabilir"` scoped `to authenticated`, `using (true)`, with no insert/update/delete policy — matches the brief's explicit note that writes go through a future `mekan_ekle` RPC (Task 6).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff alone: whether the migration is actually live on the remote `konum-sosyal` project with `postgis_version()` returning a value and `rowsecurity = true` for `mekanlar`. The brief's Step 4 called for Dashboard → SQL Editor confirmation; the implementer substituted `supabase db push` success + `--dry-run` reporting `upToDate:true`. See analysis below — I judge this adequate but it is still an indirect substitute for the brief's literal check.

## Strengths

- The migration SQL is a verbatim match of the brief's prescribed schema — no drift in column names, types, constraints, index definition, or policy text/scope. This is exactly what's wanted for a task this mechanical.
- Correctly omits insert/update/delete policies per the brief's explicit intent (writes deferred to a `security definer` RPC in Task 6), rather than inventing an ad hoc write policy.
- Migration file naming/location follows the existing project convention precisely.
- Single, minimal, self-contained change (21 lines, one file) — no scope creep.

## Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **Verification gap is real but low-risk, worth noting explicitly rather than silently accepted.** `supabase db push` executes a migration's statements sequentially and fails (and reports the error) if any statement errors — so a failed `create extension`, a failed table/index create, or a failed RLS/policy statement would have surfaced as a push failure, not a silent success. Given the push succeeded and the subsequent dry-run reported `upToDate:true` (which compares the local migration history against the remote `schema_migrations` table), this is reasonably strong indirect evidence that all statements applied. It does not, however, independently confirm current live state (e.g., it wouldn't catch someone having manually altered/dropped the table via Dashboard afterward) the way the brief's literal SQL Editor queries would. Since the implementer had no Dashboard/web access, this substitution is a defensible workaround, not negligence — but it should be flagged, not treated as equivalent, since the task's own "Expected" verification step wasn't literally satisfied.
- Extension created without a dedicated schema (`create extension postgis` lands in `public` by default) — this is a well-known Supabase Postgres lint warning ("extension in public schema"). Not a deviation from spec since the brief's SQL specifies exactly this, so this is plan-mandated and not a defect to fix in this task, just flagging for awareness in case it triggers Supabase's security advisor noise later.

## Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The migration is a precise, byte-for-byte implementation of the brief's specified schema, index, and RLS policy — correct table/column names and types, correct index, RLS enabled with a correctly scoped read-only policy and no write policies (matching the brief's explicit RPC-deferral design), correct file naming/location matching existing project convention. The only open question is verification depth: the implementer couldn't run the brief's literal Dashboard SQL Editor queries and substituted CLI push-success + dry-run evidence instead. Given that Supabase migrations fail loudly on any statement error, this substitute evidence is reasonably strong (not equivalent to, but not meaningfully weaker than, the two read-only confirmation queries the brief asked for) and doesn't block approval — it's flagged as a minor note rather than a gap requiring rework.</result>

The report substitutes `supabase db push` (initial apply) followed by `supabase db push --dry-run` returning `upToDate: true`. Given the CLI would fail loudly on a SQL syntax error, an unresolvable FK target, or a missing `postgis` extension (the `geography(point,4326)` type depends on it), a successful push is meaningful evidence the DDL executed cleanly — this is an adequate substitute for the same reasons it was adequate in Task 1's review. It does not, however, independently confirm `pg_policies` shows exactly two rows with the expected `polcmd` values; that gap is inherent to the access constraint, not a shortcut the implementer took carelessly.

### Strengths

- The SELECT policy SQL is an exact, unmodified transcription of the brief's text — no drift, no "improvements" that could have subtly changed semantics. For the single most security-critical statement in the task, doing nothing clever is the right call.
- Schema faithfully reproduces the exact column list and order from the interface spec, which matters for downstream tasks (7, 8, 9, 12, 13, 14) that will consume this table.
- The report includes an honest, explicit self-review that traces through the policy clause-by-clause rather than just asserting success, and is upfront about the Dashboard-access gap rather than glossing over it.

### RLS trace-through (Critical logic, verified)

Policy under scrutiny (diff lines 34-47):
```sql
using (
  kullanici_id = auth.uid()
  or konum is null
  or exists (
    select 1 from public.check_inler benim
    where benim.kullanici_id = auth.uid()
      and benim.mekan_id = check_inler.mekan_id
      and benim.konum is not null
      and benim.bitis_zamani &gt; now()
  )
)
```

1. **My own row** — condition 1 (`kullanici_id = auth.uid()`) is true regardless of live/memory state → **visible**. Expected: visible. ✅ Matches.
2. **Someone else's memory row** (`konum is null`) — condition 1 false, condition 2 (`konum is null`) true → **visible**. Expected: visible (past memories are public per decision #14). ✅ Matches.
3. **Someone else's live row at a venue where I also have a live check-in** — conditions 1 and 2 false; the `exists` subquery finds my own row (`benim.kullanici_id = auth.uid()`) at the same `mekan_id`, with `benim.konum is not null` and `benim.bitis_zamani &gt; now()` both true → **visible**. Expected: visible (mutual live visibility, decision #12). ✅ Matches.
4. **Someone else's live row at a venue where I do NOT have a live check-in** — condition 1 false, condition 2 false (their `konum` is not null), and the `exists` subquery finds no matching row of mine at that `mekan_id` with a live, unexpired check-in → **not visible**. Expected: not visible. ✅ Matches.

No `and`/`or` inversion, no missing `bitis_zamani &gt; now()` guard, and the exists-subquery joins correctly on `mekan_id` (not, say, `id` or `kullanici_id`). The self-referencing subquery is a standard, safe pattern here: rows belonging to `auth.uid()` always satisfy condition 1 of this same policy, so there's no circularity/blocking issue when the subquery evaluates rows of "benim" under RLS.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- The DELETE policy has no `to authenticated` role restriction (matches the brief, which also omits it). Functionally harmless — for an unauthenticated role `auth.uid()` is `null`, and `kullanici_id = null` evaluates to unknown/false, so no rows are exposed — but adding `to authenticated` would be more self-documenting and consistent with the SELECT policy's explicit role scoping. This is a brief-level nit, not something the implementer introduced.
- Only single-column indexes on `mekan_id` and `kullanici_id` exist (per brief). The SELECT policy's `exists` subquery filters on `(kullanici_id, mekan_id, konum, bitis_zamani)` together; a composite/partial index (e.g., `on check_inler (kullanici_id, mekan_id) where konum is not null`) would better serve both the RLS subquery and Task 13/14's "nearby check-ins" queries at scale. Not a spec deviation — the brief explicitly specifies only these two indexes — so this is future-optimization territory, not a defect in this task.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The migration is a precise, unmodified transcription of the brief's schema and RLS SQL. The four-case trace-through of the SELECT policy confirms it implements exactly the intended three-condition OR (own row / public memory / mutual-live-at-same-venue) with no logic inversion, no missing time guard, and a correctly-joined subquery — this is the exact defect class the review was told to scrutinize hardest, and it checks out clean. Spec compliance is complete: correct column name (`not_metni`), correct `gorunurluk` default, no insert/update policy, delete policy correctly scoped. The `supabase db push` + `--dry-run` verification is a reasonable substitute for Dashboard access given the same access constraints noted in Task 1's review — a failed apply would have surfaced as a non-`upToDate` dry-run or a push error, so the evidence, while not exhaustive, is not vacuous either.</result>

<result>### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Bucket `check-in-fotograflari` created as private: `insert into storage.buckets (id, name, public) values ('check-in-fotograflari', 'check-in-fotograflari', false);` (migration line 1-2, `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815042615_checkin_fotograflari_bucket.sql`).
- ✅ INSERT policy restricts uploads to the caller's own folder: `(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text` (line 24), matching the established Faz 1 `profil-fotograflari` convention (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260813091930_profil_fotograflari_bucket.sql:8`).
- ✅ SELECT policy mirrors `check_inler` visibility via the `fotograf` join, and the join column is correct: `c.fotograf = storage.objects.name` (line 33). This is the correct direction — `check_inler.fotograf` stores the object path, `storage.objects.name` is the object's own path, so equating them correctly resolves "which check-in row owns this file."
- ✅ Three-condition logic verified column-for-column against Task 2's actual, already-approved policy in `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815001911_check_inler.sql:18-31`. Side-by-side: `kullanici_id = auth.uid()` → `c.kullanici_id = auth.uid()`; `konum is null` → `c.konum is null`; the `benim`/mutual-live exists-subquery is reproduced verbatim, only substituting `check_inler.mekan_id` → `c.mekan_id`. This is an exact structural mirror, not a paraphrase.
- ✅ Diff matches the brief's Step 2 SQL character-for-character — no drift between prescribed and implemented SQL.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether the application code (Task 12, not yet built) will actually set `check_inler.fotograf` to the exact `storage.objects.name` value returned by the upload call. If it stores a different string (e.g., a public URL or a value with/without a leading slash), the `exists` join silently returns zero rows and photos become invisible to everyone including the owner — this is an app-layer risk to watch for in Task 12's review, not something this migration can guard against.

### Strengths

- The SELECT policy is a faithful, verifiable mirror of Task 2's approved `check_inler` policy — same predicate order, same column semantics, only the table alias changed (`c` vs. bare `check_inler`). This is exactly the "reuse proven logic" approach the brief asked for and avoids the risk of a hand-rewritten variant subtly diverging.
- Join direction is correct and unambiguous (`c.fotograf = storage.objects.name`), so the stated critical failure mode ("wrong join column making photos invisible to everyone, or visible when they shouldn't be") does not occur here.
- INSERT folder-scoping is consistent with the codebase's existing storage-policy convention (Faz 1's `profil-fotograflari`), so no new pattern was invented.
- Implementer's self-review in the report actually traces the join and three conditions correctly and doesn't overclaim beyond what CLI evidence supports.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **SELECT policy has no `to authenticated` clause, unlike the table policy it's supposed to mirror.** `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815001911_check_inler.sql:20` scopes the `check_inler` SELECT policy `to authenticated` — even "public" memory rows (`konum is null`) cannot be read by an anonymous caller at the table level. The storage policy being reviewed (`.../20260815042615_checkin_fotograflari_bucket.sql:27-46`) has no `to` clause, so it defaults to Postgres `PUBLIC`, which includes the `anon` role. Trace: for an anonymous request, `auth.uid()` is `null`, so condition 1 (`c.kullanici_id = auth.uid()`) and condition 3's `benim.kullanici_id = auth.uid()` both fail to match any row — but condition 2 (`c.konum is null`) has no dependency on `auth.uid()` at all and is satisfied regardless of caller identity. Net effect: an unauthenticated caller (using only the public anon API key, which is embedded in any published app build) *can* fetch "memory" check-in photos directly from Storage even though they cannot read the corresponding `check_inler` row. This is a genuine divergence from the thing being mirrored, not merely a stylistic gap — Task 2's design explicitly gates memory-row visibility behind authentication, and this policy doesn't. Note this is inherited unchanged from the brief's own SQL, not something the implementer introduced — but it should still be corrected (add `to authenticated`) before this ships, since it's the same severity class of "photos visible when they shouldn't be" the review was calibrated to catch, even though it doesn't stem from a join or predicate typo.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- No DELETE policy on `storage.objects` for this bucket, so a user who deletes their own `check_inler` row (via Task 2's `kendi anisini silebilir` policy) cannot also delete the underlying photo object — it becomes an orphaned, unreachable-but-undeleted file. Out of scope for this task's brief (which only specified INSERT + SELECT), so not a defect, just worth tracking for whichever task handles check-in deletion/cleanup.

### Four-case visibility trace (SELECT policy)

1. **Own photo** — `c.kullanici_id = auth.uid()` true → visible. Expected visible. Match.
2. **Someone else's memory photo** (`c.konum is null`) — condition 2 true → visible (to authenticated *and*, per the Important finding above, to anonymous callers too). Expected visible to authenticated users; the anonymous-access extension is the flagged gap.
3. **Someone else's live check-in photo at a venue where I also have a live check-in** — condition 3's `exists` subquery finds my own live (`konum is not null`, `bitis_zamani &gt; now()`) row at the same `mekan_id` → visible. Expected visible. Match.
4. **Someone else's live check-in photo at a venue where I have no live check-in** — condition 1 false, condition 2 false (it's live, `konum` not null), condition 3's subquery finds no matching `benim` row → not visible. Expected not visible. Match.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The core deliverable — the join column and the three-condition mirror of Task 2's approved policy — is implemented correctly and verified exact against the real `check_inler` migration, not just the brief's paraphrase. The bucket privacy and INSERT folder-scoping are also correct. However, the SELECT policy's missing `to authenticated` clause creates a real, non-hypothetical gap: anonymous API callers can read "memory" check-in photos that the mirrored table policy explicitly withholds from them. This should be fixed (add `to authenticated` to the SELECT — and for consistency, the INSERT — policy) before this is considered a complete and faithful mirror of Task 2's access model. It does not corrupt the mutual-live-visibility or own-row logic, so it's scoped as Important rather than Critical, but it's a genuine RLS gap and not just a nitpick.</result>

<result>## Spec Compliance

- ✅ RPC signature matches exactly: `p_lat double precision, p_lng double precision, p_yaricap_metre int default 3000, p_arama text default null` (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815080000_yakin_mekanlar_rpc.sql:1-5`).
- ✅ `ST_DWithin(konum, ST_MakePoint(p_lng, p_lat)::geography, p_yaricap_metre)` + `ad ilike '%' || p_arama || '%'` guarded by `p_arama is null or ...` — matches brief's filter logic (`...sql:10-11`).
- ✅ No `security definer` present — function defaults to invoker rights, matching the brief's explicit note that none is needed since `mekanlar` SELECT is already open (`...sql:1-16`, absence confirmed by full-file read).
- ✅ `ST_MakePoint(p_lng, p_lat)` — correct PostGIS argument order (x=lng first, y=lat second), used consistently in both the `ST_DWithin` filter and the `&lt;-&gt;` distance ordering (`...sql:10,12`).
- ✅ `Mekan` type shape matches brief's Step 5 code exactly: `{ id, ad, tur, adres, osmId, konum: { lat, lng } }` (`mobil/lib/mekan.ts:3-9`).
- ✅ `yakinMekanlariGetir(lat, lng, arama?): Promise&lt;Mekan[]&gt;` signature matches brief (`mobil/lib/mekan.ts:33-37`).
- ✅ Confirmed `yakinMekanlariGetir` does **not** pass `p_yaricap_metre` to the RPC call, relying on the SQL default of 3000 — satisfies the global "yaricap sabit 3000m, kullanici ayarlamiyor" constraint (`mobil/lib/mekan.ts:38-42`).
- ✅ Test explicitly asserts the RPC call omits `p_yaricap_metre`: `expect(supabase.rpc).toHaveBeenCalledWith('yakin_mekanlar', { p_lat: 41.015, p_lng: 28.979, p_arama: 'kafe' })` (`mobil/lib/mekan.test.ts:15-19`) — no fourth key present, confirming reliance on the SQL-side default.
- ✅ Both success and error paths tested (`mobil/lib/mekan.test.ts:12-32`, `34-37`).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: `public.mekanlar` column names/types (`ad`, `tur`, `adres`, `osm_id`, `konum`) actually match what Task 1 created, since that migration isn't in this diff.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: default `EXECUTE` grants on the new function reach the `anon`/`authenticated` PostgREST roles (no explicit `GRANT` in the migration — this matches the brief's own prescribed SQL, so not a deviation, just unverifiable statically).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: `tsconfig.json` strict mode setting (no config changes in this diff; code as written is compatible with strict mode).

## WKT Parsing Trace (`noktayiCoz`)

Input from PostgREST for Istanbul: `"POINT(28.979 41.015)"` — per WKT convention this is `POINT(lng lat)`, so lng=28.979, lat=41.015.

Regex: `/POINT\(([-\d.]+) ([-\d.]+)\)/`
- Group 1 = `"28.979"` (first number in the WKT string = lng)
- Group 2 = `"41.015"` (second number in the WKT string = lat)

Construction: `{ lng: parseFloat(eslesme[1]), lat: parseFloat(eslesme[2]) }`
→ `{ lng: 28.979, lat: 41.015 }`

This is correct — group 1 (WKT's x/lng) is assigned to the object's `lng` key, group 2 (WKT's y/lat) is assigned to `lat`. No swap. The test's expected value `konum: { lat: 41.015, lng: 28.979 }` matches this trace exactly, and `Mekan#konum` correctly represents Istanbul (~41,29), not the invalid (~29,41).

The `ST_MakePoint(p_lng, p_lat)` calls in the SQL RPC are likewise correctly ordered (PostGIS `ST_MakePoint(x, y)` = `ST_MakePoint(lng, lat)`). The full pipeline (SQL → PostgREST WKT → client parse) is consistent end-to-end with no lat/lng inversion anywhere.

## Strengths

- Zero lat/lng swap bugs across the entire pipeline (SQL point construction, WKT serialization assumption, and client-side regex parsing) — this was the single highest-risk item in the brief and it's handled correctly.
- Client function correctly omits `p_yaricap_metre`, cleanly delegating the "fixed 3000m, not user-adjustable" constraint to the SQL default rather than duplicating/hardcoding it client-side — good single-source-of-truth design.
- Clean snake_case→camelCase boundary translation (`MekanSatiri` DB row type kept private, `Mekan` is the public/camelCase type) with a well-placed comment explaining the non-obvious WKT lng/lat ordering.
- Error path throws a clear `Error` wrapping the Postgres/PostgREST error message, easy to surface in UI.
- Test mock structure is realistic (mocks only `supabase.rpc`, matches actual call site) and covers both success and error paths.

## Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None found.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None found.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `noktayiCoz`'s regex (`[-\d.]+`) is permissive — it would accept malformed strings like `"POINT(1--2.5 3..1)"` without erroring, since it doesn't enforce a proper decimal-number grammar. Not a real-world risk given PostGIS controls the output format, but a stricter pattern (e.g. `-?\d+(\.\d+)?`) would fail faster on any unexpected upstream format change.
- `data as MekanSatiri[]` is an unchecked type assertion with no runtime validation of the RPC response shape; acceptable for this codebase's apparent conventions but worth noting as the response is trusted without a schema check.
- The brief's own "Interfaces" summary line at the top uses `osm_id` for the `Mekan` type while the actual Step 5 code (and this implementation) uses `osmId` — a brief-internal inconsistency, not an implementer error, since the code faithfully follows Step 5's literal snippet.

## Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation is a verbatim, faithful realization of the brief's prescribed SQL and TypeScript, and the highest-risk item — lat/lng ordering through the WKT parsing pipeline — traces out correctly with no swap. RPC signature, security posture (invoker, no definer), the fixed-3000m-radius behavior (verified via the test's exact `toHaveBeenCalledWith` assertion omitting `p_yaricap_metre`), and the `Mekan` type shape all match the brief. Test coverage includes both success and error paths with a realistic mock. No critical or important issues found; only cosmetic/defensive-coding nits remain.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>### Finding Verdicts

**`mekan_ekle` security definer function missing `auth.uid()` null guard, allowing anon callers to bypass the daily-limit check** — ADDRESSED. Evidence: `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815091500_mekan_ekle_auth_guard.sql:114-117` (per diff line offsets) adds

```sql
begin
  if auth.uid() is null then
    raise exception 'Kimlik dogrulamasi gerekli';
  end if;

  if not ST_DWithin(...) then ...
```

as the literal first statement inside the `begin` block, before the `ST_DWithin` distance check, before the `select count(*) ... where ekleyen_kullanici = auth.uid()` daily-limit query, and before the `insert`. There is no code path in the function that reaches the count query or insert without first passing this check — the function body has no branches or early returns before it, so it is unconditional on every call. This closes the `NULL = NULL` bypass described in the finding (anon's `auth.uid()` is now rejected outright rather than silently matching zero rows).

Grant/revoke correctness: `revoke execute on function public.mekan_ekle from public, anon;` then `grant execute on function public.mekan_ekle to authenticated;`. Revoking from `public` strips the implicit grant every role (including `anon` and `authenticated`) inherits via the `PUBLIC` pseudo-role and also removes Supabase's default-privilege grant to `anon` if one existed; the explicit `anon` revoke is redundant-but-harmless belt-and-suspenders. The subsequent grant restores access only to `authenticated`, matching the finding's requested defense-in-depth. This is the correct role set for a function whose business logic (`ekleyen_kullanici = auth.uid()`) only makes sense for a logged-in caller.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

None. The fix is a `create or replace function` that reproduces the entire original body verbatim (distance check, daily-limit query, insert, return) with only the guard block inserted at the top and the grant/revoke DDL appended after `$$;`. Diffing the new migration's function body against the original in `task-6-brief.md` step 1 confirms the distance-check and daily-limit logic are byte-for-byte unchanged apart from the new guard. Client code (`mobil/lib/mekan.ts`, `mekan.test.ts`) is untouched, consistent with this being a server-only fix; the existing client-side tests mock `supabase.rpc` directly so they aren't exercising the SQL path and remain unaffected either way.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

The diff's other commit (`8ecf6e2`, edits to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-14-faz2a-mekanlar-checkin.md`) adds the identical `auth.uid() is null` guard to two not-yet-built RPCs (`check_in_yap`, `check_inden_ayril`) in the plan text only — no SQL for those exists yet. Per the controller's note this is out of scope and I did not review it further.

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.</result>

<result>## Part 1: Spec Compliance

- ✅ Auth guard `if auth.uid() is null then raise exception ...` is the first statement in the function body — `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815100000_check_in_yap_rpc.sql:16-18`.
- ✅ Venue lookup with not-found guard — `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815100000_check_in_yap_rpc.sql:20-23` (`select konum into v_mekan_konum from public.mekanlar where id = p_mekan_id`, raises `'Mekan bulunamadi'` if null).
- ✅ 500m proximity check via `ST_DWithin` — `:25`.
- ✅ Close caller's own previous active check-in, scoped by `kullanici_id = auth.uid() and konum is not null and bitis_zamani &gt; now()` — `:29-33`, placed after the distance check and before the insert.
- ✅ New check-in inserted with `bitis_zamani = now() + interval '4 hours'` and `konum = ST_MakePoint(p_lng, p_lat)::geography` — `:35-40`.
- ✅ Function returns the inserted row via `returning * into v_yeni; return v_yeni;` — `:42-43`.
- ✅ Additional requirement (revoke/grant) present and matches the Task 6 pattern — `:46-47` (`revoke execute ... from public, anon` / `grant execute ... to authenticated`).
- ✅ Client `checkInYap` maps params correctly (`p_mekan_id`, `p_lat`, `p_lng`, `p_not_metni`, `p_fotograf`) and optional args default to `null` via `??` — `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:109-124`.
- ✅ `CheckIn` type and `satiriCheckInACevir` correctly map `not_metni → notMetni` and derive `canliMi` from `konum !== null` — `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:77-107`.
- ✅ Tests cover both the success path (parameter passthrough + response shape) and the "too far" error path — `mobil/lib/checkin.test.ts`.
- No missing, extra, or misunderstood items relative to the brief or the verbal revoke/grant requirement. The implementer essentially transcribed the brief's SQL/TS verbatim and layered the revoke/grant addition on top, without scope creep.

## Strengths

- Statement order is exactly right and matches the safety-critical sequencing called out in the review brief: auth → lookup/not-found → distance check → close-previous → insert → return. No reordering, no skipped step.
- The "close previous" `UPDATE` is correctly scoped to `auth.uid()` only (never another user) and only touches rows that are still live (`konum is not null and bitis_zamani &gt; now()`), so it can't silently end an already-closed or already-expired check-in.
- `ST_DWithin(v_mekan_konum, ST_MakePoint(p_lng, p_lat)::geography, 500)` correctly compares venue geography vs. device point (not swapped), and `ST_MakePoint(lng, lat)` uses the correct PostGIS argument order (x=lng, y=lat).
- `set search_path = public` is present on the `security definer` function, consistent with hardening against search_path hijacking.
- revoke/grant correctly added after the function body, matching the Task 6 defense-in-depth pattern.
- Client-side naming/type conversion is clean and consistent with the rest of the codebase's Turkish snake_case↔camelCase convention.

## Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None found.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None found.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- No `FOR UPDATE`/locking on the close-previous `UPDATE`, so two concurrent `check_in_yap` calls from the same user (e.g. a double-tap or retry) could theoretically both pass the "no active check-in" implicit check and each insert a new row, momentarily leaving two rows with non-null `konum` before the later transaction's own future call would clean up. This isn't required by the brief and Postgres row-level locking during the `UPDATE`/`INSERT` largely mitigates it in serialized cases, but a `select ... for update` on the mekanlar/check_inler row or a partial unique index (`unique (kullanici_id) where konum is not null`) would close the race outright. Flagging as a hardening idea, not a spec violation.
- The success-path test mocks `konum: 'POINT(28.979 41.015)'`, which doesn't match the actual WKB/hex format Supabase/PostGIS would return over the wire; harmless here since `checkin.ts` only null-checks `konum`, but worth knowing this test doesn't validate real-world payload shape.

## Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The RPC implements every ordering requirement precisely — auth guard first, not-found and 500m checks before any mutation, closing the caller's previous check-in strictly scoped to their own still-live rows, insert last, 4-hour expiry, `konum` nulled (never deleted) on close. The additional revoke/grant hardening was correctly added in the same style as Task 6. Client code and tests match the brief's contract exactly (`not_metni`, `canliMi` derived from `konum !== null`). No critical or important issues found; the two minor notes (race-condition hardening, test payload fidelity) are optional follow-ups, not blockers.</result>

- ✅ Client `checkIndenAyril(checkInId: string): Promise&lt;void&gt;` matches brief signature exactly (`mobil/lib/checkin.ts:73-76`), calls `supabase.rpc('check_inden_ayril', { p_check_in_id: checkInId })`, throws `new Error(error.message)` on error.
- ✅ Task 7's existing `checkin.ts` content untouched — diff shows only an addition after `checkInYap` (context lines 61-71 unchanged: `CheckIn`/`satiriCheckInACevir` not touched by this diff at all, and `checkInYap` body is identical to before).
- ✅ Test file: import updated to include `checkIndenAyril`, two new tests added matching brief Step 5 exactly (`mobil/lib/checkin.test.ts:20,43-56`); `checkInYap`'s existing two tests (context lines 27-41) unmodified.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether `cron.schedule(...)` actually registered a job on the live database, and whether the function was actually created server-side. The report's evidence is limited to `supabase db push`'s success JSON (`"upToDate":false,...,"message":"Finished supabase db push."}` listing both migration filenames) — no SQL Editor query output or dry-run follow-up was captured, despite the brief's Step 4 explicitly calling for it.

### Strengths

- SQL in both migrations is a verbatim, faithful transcription of the brief's spec — no drift on the auth guard, scoping predicate, or cron schedule string.
- The additional revoke/grant requirement was correctly applied only to the RPC and correctly *not* applied to the cron job, showing the implementer understood the distinction between a PostgREST-exposed RPC and an internal scheduled job rather than pattern-matching blindly.
- Test additions are minimal and precisely scoped to the new function; no unrelated changes to `checkInYap`/`CheckIn` type/`satiriCheckInACevir`.
- TDD cycle (RED shown with `is not a function` failure, then GREEN with all 4 tests, then full 41-test suite) is evidenced in the report, not merely asserted.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Live verification gap.** Per the verification-depth flag: the report stops at `supabase db push` success and explicitly defers the brief's Step 4 verification queries (`select proname from pg_proc where proname = 'check_inden_ayril'` and `select jobname, schedule from cron.job where jobname = ...`) as "recommended but not blocking," without running any substitute (e.g., `--dry-run` re-push, or the queries themselves via an available SQL execution path). Push success is reasonably strong indirect evidence — `cron.schedule()` is a function call that errors rather than silently no-ops if `pg_cron` isn't properly initialized, and the CLI aborts non-zero on any statement failure — but it is not the same as positive confirmation the job row exists with the expected schedule. Given the brief made this an explicit numbered step ("Dogrula"), this should be closed out before considering the task fully done, not left as an optional follow-up.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- Commit message in the report contains a typo: "kimlik doglanan" should read "kimlik dogrulanan" (cosmetic, commit message only, not diff content).
- The cron migration is not idempotent (`cron.schedule` with no existence check would register a duplicate job if the migration file were ever re-applied outside normal `supabase migrate` bookkeeping). This mirrors the brief's SQL verbatim, so it's not an implementer deviation — flagging only as a latent fragility, not a defect to fix in this task.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The SQL for both migrations is correct and matches the brief exactly, including the security-critical pieces (auth guard as first statement, `kullanici_id = auth.uid()` scoping preventing cross-user ending of check-ins, and the cron job's system-wide no-user-filter UPDATE that nulls rather than deletes `konum`, satisfying the "never auto-deletes" global constraint). The additional revoke/grant requirement was implemented correctly and correctly scoped to only the RPC. Task 7's code is untouched, and the TDD evidence for the client function is solid. The one real gap — no independent confirmation that the cron job actually registered beyond push-success output — is a verification-depth shortfall, not a code defect, and per the calibration guidance for this review it lands as Important rather than Critical since the SQL itself is correct and inspectable as-is. ### Strengths

- Screen pattern is consistent with Task 10's `mekanlar/index.tsx` (same import order, `stiller` naming, Turkish variable names, `cihazKonumunuAl`/`yakinMekanlariGetir` usage style) — genuinely follows established convention, not just superficially similar.
- The critical location-argument requirement is tested with an exact-value assertion (`toHaveBeenCalledWith(..., {lat,lng}, {lat,lng}, ...)`), which is real behavioral verification, not a shallow `toHaveBeenCalled()` check — this is exactly the kind of test that would catch the "reversed or wrong location" failure mode called out as Critical-risk.
- Duplicate suggestions are correctly informational-only: the "Ekle" button is never disabled or hidden by `benzerMekanlar.length &gt; 0`, so the user can freely submit their own entry alongside seeing a suggestion — this matches the brief's intent for a non-blocking nudge, confirmed both by code (no conditional disable) and by test 3 not asserting the button is inert.
- Submit button correctly guards against double-submit (`disabled={gonderiliyor}`) with a `finally` block resetting state.
- `hata` is cleared at the start of every submit attempt, avoiding stale error messages lingering across retries in the success path.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Silent dead-end when device location fails or hasn't loaded yet** (`ekle.tsx:131-133`): `ekle()` unconditionally calls `setHata(null)` first, then `if (!cihazKonumu) return`. If `cihazKonumunuAl()` rejected earlier (e.g. permission denied) and set an error message, pressing "Ekle" wipes that error off screen and does nothing else — no new message, no re-fetch, no indication the button is inert. The user is left staring at a form with no error and an unresponsive button. Same silent no-op applies during the brief window before the initial location fetch resolves. Not covered by any of the three tests, and not explicitly required by the brief, but it's a real UX gap worth a one-line fix (re-set a "konum alinamadi" message, or disable the button until `cihazKonumu` is set).

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- No debounce on the duplicate-search `useEffect` (`ekle.tsx:119-129`) — fires a request on every keystroke once the name hits 2 characters, and doesn't guard against out-of-order responses from rapid typing (no request-id/cancellation check), so a slow earlier response could overwrite a newer one. Low impact given typical typing speed and small result sets, but worth noting for a later pass.
- No visible loading indicator while the initial `cihazKonumunuAl()` call is in flight (Task 10's `index.tsx` shows a "Yukleniyor..." gate before rendering the list; this screen renders the form immediately with no loading affordance), which is a minor stylistic inconsistency with the established pattern.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The brief's explicit and highest-risk requirement — passing the identical `cihazKonumu` value as both the venue-location and device-location arguments to `mekanEkle` — is implemented exactly as specified and is protected by a test that asserts exact call arguments, not just call presence. Duplicate suggestions are correctly non-blocking, server errors are surfaced generically and correctly, and the routing/loading-state/double-submit handling is clean and consistent with Task 10's established screen pattern. The one real gap (silently swallowed location-fetch error on submit attempt) is a genuine but non-blocking UX issue outside the tested/required scope of this brief; it's worth a follow-up fix but doesn't undermine spec compliance or the core distance-check integrity this gate is calibrated against.</result>

<result>### Spec Compliance

- ✅ `checkinFotografYukle` uploads to `check-in-fotograflari` bucket (mobil/lib/checkin-fotograf-yukle.ts:8) — correctly diverges from Faz 1's `profil-fotograflari`, no copy-paste bug.
- ✅ Photo-upload failure sets a warning and still proceeds: inner `try/catch` around the upload (mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:166–176) swallows the error, calls `setUyari('Fotograf yuklenemedi, notunla check-in yapildi')`, leaves `yuklenenFotoYolu` `undefined`, and execution falls through to `checkInYap(...)` (line 178) unconditionally. Check-in is not blocked.
- ✅ Network-specific error handling: `if (e instanceof TypeError &amp;&amp; e.message === 'Network request failed')` is checked first (line 181), generic fallback `setHata(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Bir sorun olustu')` is the `else` branch (line 184) — correct order, correct message text.
- ✅ Navigates to `/mekanlar/${mekanId}` on success (mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:179).
- ✅ Media restricted to photos only (`mediaTypes: ['images']`, mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx:152) — no video, matches global constraint.
- ✅ UI text is Turkish throughout.
- ✅ "Fotografi sonra ekleme" correctly not implemented — consistent with out-of-scope note.

### Strengths

- The error-handling structure exactly matches the deliberate design decision from spec self-review: the photo-upload try/catch is nested *inside* the outer try, so a photo failure cannot escape to the outer catch and incorrectly block the whole check-in. Traced this explicitly — it's correct.
- Bucket name was verified against the realistic copy-paste risk called out in the task; it's correct (`check-in-fotograflari`, not `profil-fotograflari`).
- Code matches the brief's prescribed implementation closely, with no scope creep (no "edit later" flow, no video support).
- `gonderiliyor` state correctly gated via `finally`, preventing a stuck disabled button after any code path.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **No test exercises either of the two behaviors this task exists to deliver.** The two tests in `mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].test.tsx` are: (1) a happy path where no photo is ever selected (`fotografSec` is never invoked, so `yerelFotoUri` stays `null` and the `if (yerelFotoUri)` block — including `checkinFotografYukle` — never executes), and (2) a generic `Error` rejection from `checkInYap`, which exercises the `else` fallback message, not the `TypeError('Network request failed')` branch. Neither the "photo upload fails but check-in proceeds with a warning" path nor the "network-error-specific message" path is covered by any test.
- **The implementer's report makes an inaccurate claim about test coverage.** Report line 92 states the photo-failure behavior is covered by "'not ile check-in yapar' senaryosu" — but that test never selects a photo, so `checkinFotografYukle` is never invoked and that code path is never reached. The report's "Sorunlar: Hiç sorun yok" (no issues) compounds this — a real coverage gap on the exact behavior this task was created to protect is asserted as fully verified when it isn't.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `uyari` (warning) state is never cleared at the start of `checkInYapButonu` (only `hata` is reset via `setHata(null)`). If a user retries after a prior photo-upload failure — e.g., removes the photo or a retry succeeds — the stale "Fotograf yuklenemedi..." warning remains visible.
- If `supabase.auth.getUser()` succeeds but returns no user id (`kullaniciId` falsy), the photo is silently dropped without any warning — inconsistent with the explicit warning shown for actual upload errors. Edge case, not required by the brief, but worth a follow-up.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The implementation itself is correct — bucket name, error-branch nesting/ordering, and navigation all match the brief precisely, and the core "deliberate design decision" (photo failure doesn't block check-in) is properly implemented. However, that exact behavior — along with the network-error-specific message — is left completely unverified by tests, and the implementer's self-review report asserts coverage that doesn't exist ("Bu testi: 'not ile check-in yapar' senaryosu kapsıyor" is false). Given this task's whole reason for existing is to protect two specific, explicitly-called-out behaviors, shipping without a test for either — while claiming they're tested — should be fixed before considering this task closed: add a test that selects a photo, makes `checkinFotografYukle` reject, and asserts `checkInYap` is still called with `fotograf: undefined` plus the warning renders; and a test that rejects `checkInYap` with `new TypeError('Network request failed')` and asserts the specific Turkish message appears.</result>

- (a) Mock setup genuinely simulates the failure: `ImagePicker.launchImageLibraryAsync` is mocked to resolve with `{ canceled: false, assets: [{ uri: 'file:///local/photo.jpg' }] }` — a real photo selection — then the "Fotograf ekle (opsiyonel)" button is pressed, driving `fotografSec()` in `mobil/src/app/check-in/[mekanId].tsx` (lines 171-176) to set `yerelFotoUri`. `checkinFotografYukle` is mocked to `mockRejectedValue(new Error('Upload hatasi'))`, a genuine rejection hitting the `try { ... } catch { setUyari(...) }` block at tsx lines 185-196. `checkInYap` is mocked to resolve so the flow reaches completion rather than short-circuiting on an unrelated error.

## Claude _2026-08-15 11:05:44 UTC_

Evet, şu an tasarım yapmıyoruz — bilinçli olarak. Şu ana kadar yapılan ekranlar (mekan arama, mekan ekleme, check-in, mekan detayı) işlevsel ama görsel olarak çıplak: siyah butonlar, gri çizgiler, sistem yazı tipi. Amaç önce "çalışıyor mu" sorusunu çözmek.

Tasarımı sonraya bırakmanın sebebi: ekranların hangi bilgiyi göstereceği daha yeni netleşiyor. Şimdi tasarlasak, Faz 2b'de (yakındakiler, görünürlük ayarları, engelleme) yeni ekranlar geldiğinde baştan elden geçirmek gerekirdi.

Sırası şöyle olabilir:
1. **Şimdi:** Faz 2a'nın kalan 3 görevini bitir (13 devam ediyor, sonra 14-15), sonra son inceleme.
2. **Sonra:** Uygulamayı gerçekten çalıştırıp ekranları gözle gör — hangi ekranların iyi hissettirmediğini ancak o zaman anlarız.
3. **Ondan sonra:** Tasarım geçişi. Bunun için kurulu araçlar var: `frontend-design` eklentisi (görsel yön, tipografi, "şablon gibi durmayan" tasarım) ve gstack'in `design-consultation`/`design-review` becerileri (renk paleti + font önerileri üretip önizleme çıkarıyor).

Bir alternatif de var: Faz 2b'ye geçmeden önce tasarımı yapmak, böylece 2b'nin yeni ekranları doğrudan doğru görünümle gelir. Bence bu daha mantıklı — 2a bittiğinde uygulamanın temel akışı tamam olacak, tasarım için yeterince olgun.

Sen nasıl istersin — 2a bitince tasarıma mı geçelim, yoksa önce 2b'nin işlevlerini de tamamlayıp sonra tek seferde mi tasarlayalım?

### Strengths

- The "own check-in" detection is genuinely correct: `kendiKullaniciId` comes from `supabase.auth.getUser()` (real authenticated user id), and the comparison uses the camelCase client-side field `kullaniciId` populated from `satir.kullanici_id` in `satiriGorunumeCevir` — no field mismatch, no accidental snake/camel confusion.
- `ayril()` awaits `checkIndenAyril` then awaits a full `verileriYukle()` refetch (including re-fetching `kendiKullaniciId`), so `kendiCheckIni` is recomputed fresh from freshly-fetched state on every render — no memoization or stale-closure trap. By inspection this correctly flips the button back to "Check-in yap" once the server no longer returns the user's row as live.
- Append-only edit to `checkin.ts` is real, not just claimed — confirmed directly from the diff hunks (no `-` lines touching prior exports).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None found.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Test gap: "non-matching" live-checkin case is never exercised.** The brief asks for a test proving "Check-in yap" appears in the "non-matching/no-active-checkin case." Test 3 (`mobil/src/app/mekanlar/[id].test.tsx:205-215`) only covers the *empty-list* case (`suAnBurdakileriGetir` resolves `[]`). No test covers the scenario where another user *does* have a live check-in at the venue (e.g. "Ada" with a `kullaniciId` that isn't `'kullanici-1'`) — the exact case where a wrong comparison (e.g. `!!c.kullaniciId` or always-true) would slip through undetected. The test only asserts `checkIndenAyril` was called with the right id — it never asserts the button text reverts to "Check-in yap" after refetch, and the current mock setup couldn't catch a regression there even if one existed. The code path looks correct by inspection, but the "does it correctly reload and switch back" requirement from the brief is unverified by the test suite.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- No error handling around `verileriYukle()` — if `suAnBurdakileriGetir`/`mekanAnilariniGetir`/`getUser()` reject, it's an unhandled promise rejection with no user-facing feedback.
- The "Ayrildim"/"Check-in yap" button isn't disabled while `ayril()` is in flight, so a fast double-press could fire `checkIndenAyril` twice (idempotency depends on the RPC, outside this task's scope).

1. **Gap 1:** No test covered the case where another user has a live check-in (only the empty-list case was tested, risking that a broken "is this my own check-in" comparison would slip through).

---

## Finding Verdicts

### Finding 1: Another User's Live Check-In Test — **ADDRESSED**

**Location:** diff lines 50-62 (new test "baskasinin aktif check-ini varsa check-in yap butonu gosterir ve ayrildim butonu yoktur")

**Evidence:**
- Line 52: Mock returns check-in with `kullaniciId: 'kullanici-2'` 
- Logged-in user mock (test setup, line 147): `'kullanici-1'` — genuinely different IDs ✓
- Line 61: **Positive assertion** — `expect(screen.getByText('Check-in yap')).toBeTruthy()` confirms button IS shown ✓
- Line 62: **Negative assertion** — `expect(screen.queryByText('Ayrildim')).toBeNull()` confirms "Ayrildim" is NOT present ✓

The test correctly exercises the scenario where another user occupies the venue, ensuring the button comparison logic (line 249 of [id].tsx: `const kendiCheckIni = suAnBurdakiler.find((c) =&gt; c.kullaniciId === kendiKullaniciId)`) properly rejects the other user's check-in.

### Finding 2: Post-"Ayrildim" Button Switch-Back — **ADDRESSED**

**Evidence:**
- Lines 30–32: `mockResolvedValueOnce([...])` — first call returns user's own live check-in ✓
- Line 33: `mockResolvedValueOnce([])` — second call returns empty array ✓

- Lines 45–47: **Button switch assertion** — `expect(screen.getByText('Check-in yap')).toBeTruthy()` after pressing "Ayrildim", confirming the refetch updates the UI correctly ✓

The refetch path (line 253 of [id].tsx: `await verileriYukle()`) will call `suAnBurdakileriGetir` a second time, getting the empty array, triggering a re-render where `kendiCheckIni` becomes undefined and the button switches to "Check-in yap".

- **5 düzeltme turu** gerekti; ikisi gerçek güvenlik açığıydı:
  - Storage politikalarında `to authenticated` eksikti (kimliksiz kullanıcı anı fotoğraflarını okuyabiliyordu)
  - `mekan_ekle` RPC'sinde `auth.uid()` null kontrolü yoktu (kimliksiz çağrı günlük limiti atlatabiliyordu) — aynı koruma diğer iki RPC'ye de önden eklendi
- Kalanlar test kapsamı boşluklarıydı, hepsi kapatıldı

<result>## Review Summary

I read the full spec, the full 2206-line diff (both passes), the progress ledger, and the pre-existing Faz 1 `profiller` migration (not in the diff but load-bearing for a cross-cutting question). I also ran three read-only checks against the actual linked Supabase project (`supabase db query --linked` for FK constraints and RLS policies, plus one anon-key REST call) to verify a suspected defect empirically rather than by inference. No writes, no mutations, no schema changes were made — all three checks were `SELECT`/read-only.

### End-to-End Journey Trace

Traced: index → mekanlar (arama) → mekan seç → mekan detayı → check-in yap (not+foto) → detay ekranına dön (canlı görünür) → "Ayrıldım" veya süre dolumu (cron) → anı bölümüne düşer → profil/anılar → haritada aç → sil.

Everything **except viewing other people's names on the mekan detail screen** lines up cleanly: field names agree end-to-end (`not_metni`, `mekan_id`, `konum`↔`canliMi`), routing targets match push/replace calls exactly, the single-active-check-in close-then-insert ordering is correct, and the profile-memories path (venue coords only, never user GPS) is correct.

However, I found a **critical, verified break** in the shared-visibility half of the journey (see Critical Issues below): `mekanlar/[id].tsx`'s "Su an burada" and "Anılar" sections cannot load in production.

### Privacy Model Assessment

Holds up well on the axis the spec cares about (device GPS never durably stored/shown):
- `check_inler.konum` is nulled by `check_inden_ayril` and by the `pg_cron` job (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815120000_ani_donusumu_cron.sql`) — row survives, coordinate doesn't.
- No client code (`mobil/lib/checkin.ts`) ever surfaces the raw `konum` WKT to the UI — only a derived `canliMi` boolean.
- `kendiAnilariniGetir` correctly returns `mekanlar.konum` (venue's fixed pin), never `check_inler.konum`, which is null anyway by the time it's a memory.
- Storage RLS for check-in photos references `check_inler.fotograf`/visibility, `to authenticated` (already fixed per-task).
- Deleting a memory (`aniyiSil`) orphans the Storage photo object (no DELETE policy on the bucket), but since the SELECT policy requires a matching `check_inler` row to exist, the orphan becomes unreadable to everyone including the original owner — a storage-cost issue, not a leak. Confirms the ledger's deferred-minor ruling was right.
- Minor unverified consideration: `checkinFotografYukle` uploads the picked image's raw bytes; if the source photo carries EXIF GPS metadata, that survives upload untouched by app logic. Speculative (not exercised in this diff, no test either way) — flagging as a note, not a defect.

### Visibility Model Assessment

**This is where the critical defect lives.** The spec requires "mutual live visibility" (checked-in users see each other at the same venue) and "public memories" (past check-ins visible to all), both enforced by Postgres RLS with client code doing no additional filtering. The RLS design for `check_inler` itself is correct (already verified per-task, four-case truth table holds, confirmed still live via `pg_policies` query).

But the query that *reads* that data joins in the check-in author's display name via `profiller(ad)`:

```
mobil/lib/checkin.ts:598  .select('id, mekan_id, kullanici_id, not_metni, fotograf, olusturma_zamani, bitis_zamani, konum, profiller(ad)')  (suAnBurdakileriGetir)
mobil/lib/checkin.ts:608  .select('id, mekan_id, kullanici_id, not_metni, fotograf, olusturma_zamani, bitis_zamani, konum, profiller(ad)')  (mekanAnilariniGetir)
```

There is **no foreign key** between `check_inler` and `profiller` — both independently reference `auth.users(id)` (confirmed live via `pg_constraint`: `check_inler_kullanici_id_fkey → auth.users`, `profiller_id_fkey → auth.users`, nothing connecting the two directly). PostgREST requires a direct FK between the two embedded tables to resolve `table(nested_table)` syntax. I confirmed this empirically against the live project:

```
curl .../rest/v1/check_inler?select=id,profiller(ad)&amp;limit=1
→ {"code":"PGRST200","message":"Could not find a relationship between 'check_inler' and 'profiller' in the schema cache"}
```

Even if that relationship existed, it wouldn't help: `profiller`'s SELECT RLS policy (from Faz 1, `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260813115636_profiller.sql:12-14`, confirmed still live via `pg_policies`) is `using (auth.uid() = id)` — **self-only**. No policy in this diff or any prior migration allows one user to read another user's `ad`. Faz 1 was built for a single-user-sees-own-profile world; Faz 2a's entire premise (see other people's names at a shared venue) needs it loosened, and no task in the 15-task plan touched `profiller`'s RLS.

**Impact:** every call to `suAnBurdakileriGetir` or `mekanAnilariniGetir` throws against the real backend. `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/[id].tsx:19-25` (`verileriYukle`) has no try/catch around the `Promise.all` that calls both, so the rejection is unhandled — the screen silently stays on its initial empty state forever ("Su an kimse yok" / "Henuz bir ani yok"), **falsely implying no one is there and nothing has ever happened at the venue**, rather than surfacing any error. This is the one screen whose entire job is to show other users' presence and history — it cannot do so.

This was invisible to the 66/66 green test suite because every test mocks the Supabase client (`jest.mock('./supabase', ...)`) and stubs `profiller: { ad: 'Ada' }` directly — none of them exercise real PostgREST relationship resolution or real RLS. This is exactly the class of defect a per-task, mock-based review cannot see, and exactly what Task 15's deferred "no one has ever run this in a browser" note foreshadowed.

`kendiAnilariniGetir` (profile's own memories, `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:622` joining `mekanlar(ad, konum)`) is unaffected — `check_inler.mekan_id → mekanlar.id` is a real FK and `mekanlar`'s SELECT policy is `to authenticated using (true)`. So the "Anılarim" screen works; only the shared/public venue-detail view is broken.

### Consistency &amp; Duplication

Done well: naming is uniform across all 15 tasks (`satiriXCevir` converter pattern, snake_case DB ↔ camelCase TS, `not_metni` used consistently with no stray typos), the `noktayiCoz`/`mesafeMetre`/`cihazKonumunuAl` DRY refactor (Task 14) left exactly one definition in `lib/konum.ts`, all three `security definer` RPCs got the same `auth.uid()` guard + revoke/grant hardening, and all six new screens follow the same loading/hata-state/StyleSheet structure.

Gaps found only at the whole-branch level:
- **Missing error handling recurs across screens**, not just the one instance Task 13's reviewer deferred: `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/[id].tsx` (`verileriYukle`), `mobil/src/app/profil/anilar.tsx:8-13` (`anilariYukle`), and `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/index.tsx:27-31` (`aramaDegisti`, the post-initial-load search path) all call data-fetching functions with no try/catch, unlike their own initial-load paths which do have one. Given the Critical finding above, this pattern is what turns "the query throws" into "the screen silently lies."
- **Network-error friendliness is inconsistent.** Only `check-in/[mekanId].tsx:1110-1112` special-cases `TypeError('Network request failed')` into a Turkish message ("Internet baglantisi yok, tekrar dene"). `mekanlar/ekle.tsx` and `mekanlar/index.tsx` hit the same failure mode but would surface the raw English exception text via the generic `e.message` fallback.

### Missing / Gaps

- **Offline queueing not implemented.** The spec's error table (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-14-faz2a-mekanlar-checkin-design.md:188`) states "Ag yok → Check-in kuyrukta bekler, baglanti gelince gonderilir" (queued, auto-sent on reconnect). The actual behavior is a one-shot attempt with a friendly error message and manual retry — no queue, no auto-resend. No task in the plan implemented this; it isn't called out anywhere as a deliberate, documented scope cut (unlike other spec deviations in this project, which get logged in CLAUDE.md/konusma-gunlugu.md per this repo's own convention).
- **"Fotograf sonra eklenebilir"** (spec: photo can be added after a failed upload) has no corresponding affordance — check-in proceeds without the photo permanently, no edit-check-in-photo path exists.
- Everything else the spec requires for Faz 2a (search, add-venue with 200m/duplicate/daily-limit guards, check-in with 500m guard and single-active-checkin closing, live mutual visibility RLS, cron-driven memory conversion, profile memories with delete) is present and, aside from the profiller join, wired correctly.

### Triage of Deferred Minors

- Task 1 (a) dashboard-verification substitute, (b) postgis in `public` schema — **fine-as-is**, no code impact, plan-mandated.
- Task 2 (a) DELETE policy missing `to authenticated` — **fine-as-is**, confirmed harmless (anon's `auth.uid()` is null, matches zero rows, verified via live `pg_policies`). (b) single-column indexes — **fine-as-is**, future scaling concern only.
- Task 3 storage bucket has no DELETE policy (orphans photos on memory deletion) — **fine-as-is**, confirmed it's a storage-cost issue only, not a leak (orphan becomes unreadable to everyone once its `check_inler` row is gone).
- Task 5 permissive `noktayiCoz` regex, unchecked casts, brief-internal doc naming inconsistency — **fine-as-is**, low risk, data always originates from trusted own-DB round-trips.
- Task 6 auth-guard ruling (Minor→Important, fixed) — **agree**, correctly caught and closed.
- Task 6 no distinct error codes for RPC failures — **fine-as-is**, client only branches on message text today.
- Task 7 (a) no row-level locking on close-previous-checkin (race could transiently leave two live rows) — **fine-as-is** for merge, but worth a follow-up partial-unique-index if it's ever observed in practice. (b) test mocks WKT as plain text, not real wire format — **fine-as-is** individually, but I'll note it's a symptom of the same "tests never touch real Postgres/PostgREST" gap that let the Critical finding through.
- Task 8 Important (live confirmation via `supabase db query`) — **agree**, well-verified with positive evidence. Minors (cron non-idempotent, commit typo) — **fine-as-is**.
- Task 9 ruling (Approved despite Important label, no actionable fix for untested osmium plumbing) — **agree**, reasonable; genuinely nothing to fix without a real 2GB extract. Minors (report overclaim wording, no upsert/idempotency for re-running the loader) — **fine-as-is** for merge; the re-run one is worth a note for whoever owns the script next.
- Task 10 cosmetic minor — **fine-as-is**.
- Task 11 debounce/loading-indicator minors — **fine-as-is**, low-traffic screens.
- Task 12 stale-`uyari`-state and silent-photo-drop-on-missing-user-id minors — **fine-as-is**, low impact, edge cases.
- Task 13 "Ayrildim"/"Check-in yap" double-press-not-disabled — **fine-as-is**, effectively idempotent.
- Task 13 **"no error handling around `verileriYukle()`"** — I disagree with leaving this as a pure defer. It's not just a coverage gap; it's the specific mechanism that turns the profiller join failure into a silent, permanently-wrong empty state instead of a visible, debuggable error. **Recommend fixing alongside the Critical fix below**, not as a separate future pass.
- Task 14 delete-RLS cross-task closure ruling — **agree**, correctly verified against Task 2's policy.
- Task 15 "manual `npx expo start --web` walkthrough was skipped" — I disagree with leaving this purely deferred to "post-final-review handoff." This is precisely the step that would have caught the Critical finding (a two-browser-tab check-in test would have shown the venue screen failing to load anyone). **Recommend it be performed as verification once the Critical fix lands**, not indefinitely deferred.

No controller ruling struck me as substantively wrong — Task 6's and Task 8's escalations were the right call, and Task 9's non-actionable-finding ruling was reasonable. The gap that slipped through is one no per-task review (all of which reason about diffs and mocked tests) could realistically have caught; it required cross-referencing this diff against a pre-existing Faz 1 table's RLS and against the live database directly, which is exactly this final review's job.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
1. **`mekanlar/[id].tsx`'s "Su an burada" and "Anılar" sections cannot load — verified live.** `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:595-613` (`suAnBurdakileriGetir`, `mekanAnilariniGetir`) select `profiller(ad)` embedded from `check_inler`, but no FK connects the two tables (confirmed via `pg_constraint` on the linked project) and PostgREST's schema cache rejects the query (`PGRST200`, confirmed via a live REST call). Even with a relationship fixed, `profiller`'s SELECT RLS (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260813115636_profiller.sql:12-14`, self-only, confirmed live via `pg_policies`) blocks reading anyone else's name. **Fix requires two changes**: (a) add a migration creating an FK from `check_inler.kullanici_id` to `public.profiller(id)` so PostgREST can resolve the embed, and (b) add/loosen a `profiller` SELECT policy so other authenticated users can read at least the `ad` column (e.g., `to authenticated using (true)` on `ad`, or a policy scoped to check-in co-visibility if tighter exposure is wanted). Without both, the core "see who else is checked in / see venue memories" feature of this entire phase does not function, and fails silently rather than with a visible error (see Task 13 minor above).

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. Add try/catch (or `.catch()`) around the data-loading calls in `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/[id].tsx:26-34`, `mobil/src/app/profil/anilar.tsx:8-13`, and `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/index.tsx:27-31`, so a query failure surfaces to the user instead of leaving a permanently misleading empty state. Directly relevant given the Critical finding above.
2. Offline queueing (spec's "Ag yok → kuyrukta bekler, baglanti gelince gonderilir") is not implemented anywhere in the 15 tasks; current behavior is a one-shot attempt with manual retry. Either implement minimal queue/retry for `checkInYap`, or explicitly log this as an accepted scope simplification (per this project's own convention of recording spec deviations) so it isn't silently lost.
3. Once the Critical fix lands, perform the manual `npx expo start --web` two-user walkthrough that was deferred by Task 15 — it is the verification step that would have caught this class of bug, and it still hasn't been done by anyone.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. Network-error message inconsistency: only `check-in/[mekanId].tsx` translates `TypeError('Network request failed')` into a friendly Turkish message; `mekanlar/ekle.tsx` and `mekanlar/index.tsx` would show the raw English exception text.
2. "Fotograf sonra eklenebilir" (spec) has no follow-up UI to add a photo after a failed upload; check-in permanently proceeds without it.
3. `check_in_yap` RPC (`mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815100000_check_in_yap_rpc.sql`) doesn't verify `p_fotograf` was actually uploaded by the calling user before storing it on the row — low-risk edge case given photo paths are timestamp-based and not attacker-controlled in the UI, but worth noting for defense-in-depth.
4. Possible EXIF GPS metadata surviving in uploaded check-in photos — unverified either way in this diff, flagging as a follow-up question rather than a confirmed defect.

### Assessment

**Merge readiness:** Needs fixes.

**Reasoning:** The end-to-end plumbing, the RLS design for check-ins, the auth-guard hardening across all three RPCs, and the GPS-never-durably-stored privacy invariant are all genuinely solid — this is a well-executed 15-task build on those axes, and the per-task review process caught and fixed two real security defects correctly. But the whole-branch view surfaced one Critical, empirically-confirmed defect that no per-task review could see: the venue detail screen's two core queries (`suAnBurdakileriGetir`, `mekanAnilariniGetir`) fail against the real database because of a missing FK relationship and a Faz-1-era RLS policy that was never updated for Faz 2a's requirement to show other users' names, and the failure is silent rather than visible. This breaks the "mutual live visibility" and "public memories" feature — the actual stated purpose of this phase — in production, despite a fully green 66-test suite (which never touches real Postgres/PostgREST behavior). This must be fixed, along with adding basic error surfacing, before merge; the offline-queue gap is worth a decision (fix or explicitly accept) but is not blocking on its own.</result>

<result>## Finding Verdicts

**Finding 1 (Critical) — ADDRESSED**

- `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815130000_check_inler_kullanici_adi.sql:1-2` adds `check_inler.kullanici_adi text`.
- `mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815140000_check_in_yap_kullanici_adi.sql:1-50` replaces `check_in_yap` via `create or replace function`.
- **Line-by-line body diff against the prior version** (`c54e7d1:mobil/supabase/migrations/20260815100000_check_in_yap_rpc.sql`): auth guard (`if auth.uid() is null...`), venue lookup + not-found check, `ST_DWithin` 500m check, and the close-previous-active-checkin `update` are byte-identical. The only changes: (1) `v_kullanici_adi text` added to `declare`; (2) `select ad into v_kullanici_adi from public.profiller where id = auth.uid();` inserted between the distance check and the update (read-only against `profiller`, touches no `check_inler` row, so it cannot reorder-break anything); (3) `insert` column list and `values` gain `kullanici_adi` / `v_kullanici_adi`. `returning * into v_yeni; return v_yeni;` unchanged. Body reproduction confirmed correct.
- Missing-profile handling: `select ... into v_kullanici_adi` on zero rows leaves the variable `null` without raising (this is standard plpgsql `select into` semantics — no `strict`, so no exception on no-match). Correct per spec.
- No FK added: the diff's full migration set contains no `alter table ... add constraint` touching `check_inler`/`profiller`; report's live query (`pg_constraint` on `check_inler`) shows only the two pre-existing FKs (`kullanici_id`→auth.users, `mekan_id`→mekanlar) plus PK. `profiller` RLS untouched — no migration in the diff references `profiller`'s policies at all.
- Client: `mobil/lib/checkin.ts:58` (`kullaniciAdi: string | null`), `:60` (`CheckInSatiriProfilli` now `{ kullanici_id, kullanici_adi }`), `:66` (`kullaniciAdi: satir.kullanici_adi`), `:73` and `:83` (`.select('..., kullanici_adi')`, `profiller(ad)` gone). Grep for `profiller` across `checkin.ts`/`checkin.test.ts` returns nothing — confirmed no residual references.

**Finding 2 (Important) — ADDRESSED**

- `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/[id].tsx:253-265` — `verileriYukle`'s `Promise.all` body now wrapped in try/catch, sets `hata` state, cleared on success; rendered at `:281`.
- `mobil/src/app/profil/anilar.tsx:418-426` — `anilariYukle` wrapped in try/catch, `hata` state added and rendered at `:445`.
- `mobil/src/app/mekanlar/index.tsx:34-40` — `aramaDegisti` (search-as-you-type) now wrapped in try/catch, reusing the pre-existing `hata` state/render (`:44`) that the initial-load path (`:17-26`) already used.
- Three new tests added, each mocking a rejection and asserting the error text renders (`[id].test.tsx:216-225`, `anilar.test.tsx:391-397`, `index.test.tsx:323-339`) — these assert real behavior (element-not-found → element-found via `waitFor`), not vacuous checks.

## New Breakage in the Fix Diff

None found. The RPC reorder (name lookup inserted before the close-previous-checkin update) is a read-only side query with no interaction with `check_inler`, so it can't affect the update/insert/return sequence. `CheckInGorunumu.kullaniciAdi` widening to `string | null` is a compile-safe change; the one consumer (`&lt;Text&gt;{item.kullaniciAdi}&lt;/Text&gt;` in `[id].tsx`) tolerates `null` (renders empty) without crashing.

## Out-of-Scope Observations

- `mekanlar/index.tsx`'s error render (`if (hata) return &lt;Text.../&gt;`) fully replaces the screen rather than overlaying, same pattern as the pre-existing initial-load error handling — a search-time failure discards already-rendered results from view. This predates the fix (same pattern was already used for the initial-load path) and isn't part of Finding 2's ask; not blocking.
- `docs/konusma-gunlugu.md` changes (commit `992b69d`) correctly out of scope per instructions, not reviewed for content.

## Verdict

**Fix wave:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage
**Merge readiness:** Ready</result>

## Verdiğim kararlar

Bunlar senin adına aldığım kararlar — yanlış bulduğun varsa geri alalım:

1. **İzole worktree kurulmadı.** `mobil/.env` ve Supabase CLI bağlantısı gitignored olduğu için worktree'de uygulama çalışmazdı. *Yanlışsa maliyeti:* iş paylaşılan dalda kaldı, istersen herhangi bir commit'ten dallanabiliriz.

2. **Task 3 — Storage politikalarına `to authenticated` eklendi.** İnceleme, kimliksiz kullanıcının anı fotoğraflarını doğrudan Storage'dan okuyabildiğini buldu. Plan metninden gelen bir açıktı. Faz 1'in `profil-fotograflari` bucket'ında aynı açık duruyor — kapsam dışı bıraktım, ileriye not.

3. **Task 6 — `auth.uid()` null koruması.** Otomatik güvenlik taraması, kimliksiz çağrının günlük mekan ekleme limitini atlatabildiğini buldu (SQL'de `x = NULL` hiç eşleşmediği için sayım hep sıfır). Düzelttim ve aynı korumayı henüz yazılmamış iki RPC'nin plan metnine de önden ekledim.

4. **Task 9 — OSM betiği için "gerçek veriyle test edilmedi" bulgusu düzeltme turuna sokulmadı.** Kod hatası değil, ortam kısıtı (2 GB dosya). Betiğin ilk gerçek çalıştırması zaten bu testin kendisi.

5. **Son inceleme geçici olarak Sonnet'e düşürüldü** — sen Opus dediğinde geri aldım.

6. **Kritik hatanın çözümünde `profiller` RLS'ini gevşetmeyi reddettim.** İnceleme onu öneriyordu ama Postgres RLS satır düzeyinde: `ad`'ı açan her politika `dogum_tarihi`, `biyografi` ve `fotograflar`'ı da açardı. Bir hata düzeltmesinin içine gizlenmiş Faz 2b kapsamlı bir gizlilik değişikliği olurdu. Bunun yerine adı `check_inler`'e kopyaladım. *Tavizi:* adını değiştiren kullanıcının eski check-in'lerinde eski ad kalır.

İyi soru — ve cevabı önemli, çünkü yanlış tarafa koymak mağaza reddine yol açabiliyor.

**Önerim: ayrı bir yerden, uygulamanın içinden değil.** Üç gerekçe:

1. **Mağaza incelemesi.** Tüketici uygulamasının içine gizlenmiş yönetici işlevi, App Store ve Play Store incelemelerinde takılabilen bir şey. "Gizli işlevsellik" başlığı altında reddedilen uygulamalar var. Kod herkesin telefonuna iniyor, sadece sen göremiyorsun.

2. **Bir hata olursa yetki sızar.** Yönetici ekranı uygulamanın içindeyse, onu koruyan tek şey bir rol kontrolü. O kontrolde bir hata olursa — ya da biri uygulamayı tersine mühendislikle incelerse — sıradan kullanıcı yönetici işlevlerine ulaşır. Ayrı bir yerde duran panel için böyle bir risk yok, kod kullanıcının cihazında hiç bulunmuyor.

3. **Pratiklik.** Şikayet edilen fotoğrafları ve metinleri telefon ekranında incelemek kötü bir deneyim. Moderasyon, yan yana bilgi görmeyi gerektiren bir iş.

Sıra doğal olarak bu.
